<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As
of December&nbsp;31, 2005, the Company has net operating losses of approximately $122.2&nbsp;million for tax purposes which will be available to offset future
taxable income. If not used, these carryforwards will expire between 2018 and 2025. The Company's state net operating loss carryforwards will also be subject to expiration over varying years. To the
extent that net operating loss carryforwards, when realized, relate to non-qualified stock option deductions, the resulting benefits will be credited to stockholders' equity. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In
the event that the Federal net operating loss is subject to Section&nbsp;382 of the Internal Revenue Code the use of the net operating loss carryforward could be limited in any one
year. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>10.&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Redeemable Convertible Preferred Stock  </B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As of December&nbsp;31, 2005, the Company had authorized 30,419,811 shares of preferred stock at $0.01 par value. The Board of Directors has the authority to
issue shares and to fix voting privileges, dividend rates, conversion privileges, and any other rights of the preferred stock. The currently authorized shares of preferred stock are further designated
as 18,000,000 shares of Series&nbsp;E redeemable convertible preferred stock ("Series&nbsp;E preferred stock"); 6,419,811 shares of Series&nbsp;E-1 redeemable convertible preferred
stock ("Series&nbsp;E-1 preferred stock"); and 6,000,000 shares of Series&nbsp;F redeemable convertible preferred stock ("Series&nbsp;F preferred stock"). </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Each
share of preferred stock is convertible into one third of a share of common stock at the option of the holder and converts automatically upon a qualified initial public offering of
common stock&#151;as defined per the terms and conditions of the preferred stock purchase agreements. The conversion ratio is </FONT></P>

<P ALIGN="CENTER"><FONT SIZE=2>F-25</FONT></P>

<HR NOSHADE>
<P style='page-break-before:always'></p>
<!-- ZEQ.=4,SEQ=104,EFW="2168369",CP="TRAFFIC.COM, INC.",DN="1",CHK=245107,FOLIO='F-25',FILE='DISK133:[06NYC1.06NYC2221]JP2221A.;5',USER='LBURNET',CD='24-MAR-2006;08:49' -->
<A NAME="page_jp2221_1_26"> </A>
<BR>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>subject
to change based on certain dilution events. The holders of the Series&nbsp;E, Series&nbsp;E-1, and Series&nbsp;F preferred stock, voting as a separate class, are entitled to
elect three of the seven directors. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In
March&nbsp;2003, the Company entered into an agreement with a group of investors to sell 14,425,000 shares of Series&nbsp;E preferred stock. The sale resulted in proceeds to the
Company of $28.9&nbsp;million, which included converted debt of $1.4&nbsp;million. As an inducement to participate in the Series&nbsp;E financing, the Company made available 6,419,811 shares of
Series&nbsp;E-1 preferred stock to its existing holders of preferred stock. These shares were distributed to existing investors that participated in the Series&nbsp;E round (based on
the percentage of their investment in the Series&nbsp;E round) in exchange for shares of Series&nbsp;A, B, C and D Preferred Stock. Based on a fair value for the Series&nbsp;E-1
convertible preferred stock of $0.56 per share, the difference between the carrying value of the Series&nbsp;A through D convertible preferred stock ($15.3&nbsp;million) that has been derecognized
and the fair value of the Series&nbsp;E-1 convertible preferred stock ($3.6&nbsp;million) that has been recognized, of $11.7&nbsp;million, has been recognized as an addition to net
income to arrive at net income attributable to common stockholders in the accompanying statement of operations for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2003. The Company is recording an accretion
adjustment in each accounting period subsequent to initial recognition of the Series&nbsp;E-1 preferred stock to reflect the return to the preferred stockholders. </FONT></P>


<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As
a result of the March&nbsp;2003 Series&nbsp;E financing, 873,671 shares of Series&nbsp;A preferred stock were exchanged for 291,223 shares of common stock and 600,000 shares of
Series&nbsp;A preferred stock warrants were
exchanged for 40,000 common stock warrants. The 4,824,563 outstanding shares of Series&nbsp;B preferred stock were exchanged for 321,637 shares of common stock and 1,315,789 outstanding shares of
Series&nbsp;C preferred stock were exchanged for 438,596 shares of common stock. The Company repurchased 2,595,585 shares of Series&nbsp;D preferred stock, which had a carrying value of
$34.8&nbsp;million including cumulative dividends of $5.6&nbsp;million, for $9.4&nbsp;million. The $25.4&nbsp;million difference between the carrying value and repurchase amount was recognized
as income in net income attributable to common stockholders in the accompanying statement of operations for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2003. In addition, 5,324 shares of Series&nbsp;D
preferred stock were exchanged for 354 shares of common stock. Preferred stockholders of each series that did not participate in the Series&nbsp;E financing round were subject to conversion of their
preferred stock to common stock at a conversion ratio ranging from 1 for 1 to 1 for 5. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In
December&nbsp;2003, warrants for 1,840,094 shares of Series&nbsp;E preferred stock were exercised by the Company's Senior Secured lender. Upon exercise of the warrants, the
carrying value of $3.7&nbsp;million was reclassified to the redemption value of the Series&nbsp;E preferred stock. </FONT></P>


<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In
September&nbsp;2005, the Company issued and sold 5,042,090 shares of Series&nbsp;F preferred stock at a price of $3.00 per share for proceeds of $15.1&nbsp;million. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The
Series&nbsp;E preferred stockholders receive noncumulative dividends at an annual rate of 8%, if declared. Each holder of the Series&nbsp;E preferred stock is entitled to one
vote for each share of common stock into which such share of Series&nbsp;E preferred stock is convertible. The Series&nbsp;E preferred stock is redeemable by the holder in one-third
increments on the fifth, sixth, and seventh anniversary of the first issuance of Series&nbsp;F preferred stock at an amount equal to the original purchase price ($2.00 per share) plus any accrued
but unpaid dividends. In the event of liquidation, after payment of the Series&nbsp;F liquidation preference, the Series&nbsp;E preferred stock is entitled to receive the greater of $4.00 per
share or the proceeds they would receive on an as converted basis into common stock. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The
outstanding shares of preferred stock were converted into 9,242,312&nbsp;shares of common stock in accordance with their terms. </FONT></P>

<P ALIGN="CENTER"><FONT SIZE=2>F-26</FONT></P>

<HR NOSHADE>
<P style='page-break-before:always'></p>
<!-- ZEQ.=5,SEQ=105,EFW="2168369",CP="TRAFFIC.COM, INC.",DN="1",CHK=324069,FOLIO='F-26',FILE='DISK133:[06NYC1.06NYC2221]JP2221A.;5',USER='LBURNET',CD='24-MAR-2006;08:49' -->
<!-- THIS IS THE END OF A COMPOSITION COMPONENT -->

<P><FONT SIZE=2><A
NAME="page_jq2221_1_27"> </A> </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>11.&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Stockholders' Deficit  </B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The
Series&nbsp;E-1 preferred stock receives noncumulative dividends at an annual rate of 8%, if declared. Each holder of the Series&nbsp;E-1 preferred stock
is entitled to one vote for each share of common stock into which such share of Series&nbsp;E-1 preferred stock is convertible. The Series&nbsp;E-1 preferred stock is
redeemable by the holder in one-third increments on the fifth, sixth, and seventh anniversary of the first issuance of Series&nbsp;F preferred stock at an amount equal to the original
purchase price plus any accrued but unpaid dividends. In the event of liquidation, after payment of the Series&nbsp;F and Series&nbsp;E liquidation preference, the Series&nbsp;E-1
preferred stock is entitled to receive the greater of $1.56 per share plus an annual 8% dividend, or the proceeds they would receive on an as converted basis into common stock. </FONT></P>


<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The
Series&nbsp;F preferred stockholders receive noncumulative dividends at an annual rate of 8%, if declared. Each holder of the Series&nbsp;F preferred stock is entitled to one
vote for each share of common stock into which such share of Series&nbsp;F preferred stock is convertible. The Series&nbsp;F preferred stock is redeemable by the holder in one-third
increments on the fifth, sixth, and seventh anniversary of the first issuance of the Series&nbsp;F preferred stock at an amount equal to the original purchase price ($3.00 per share) plus any
accrued but unpaid dividends. In the event of liquidation, the Series&nbsp;F preferred stockholders are entitled to receive the greater of $4.00 per share or the proceeds they would receive on an as
converted basis into common stock. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;No
dividends have been declared on any of the series of preferred stock. The preferred stock is redeemable in installments of $21.8&nbsp;million in September&nbsp;2010,
$22.3&nbsp;million in 2011, and $22.8&nbsp;million in 2012. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;At
the closing of the Company's initial public offering in January&nbsp;2006, each share of preferred stock was converted into one third of a share of common stock. The outstanding
shares of preferred stock were converted into 9,242,312 shares of common stock in accordance with their terms. </FONT></P>


<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>Common Stock  </B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Holders of common stock, voting together with the holders of the Series&nbsp;E, Series&nbsp;E-1, and Series&nbsp;F preferred stock, are entitled
to elect three of the seven directors of the Company. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>Stock Options  </B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Prior to January&nbsp;2006, the Company had two stock option plans: the 1999 Long-term Incentive Plan ("Incentive Plan") and the 1999 Nonemployees'
Stock Plan ("Nonemployee Plan"). The Incentive Plan provides for the issuance of options, stock appreciation rights, restricted stock, deferred stock, and other stock-based awards to officers and key
employees of the Company. The Nonemployee Plan provides for the issuance of options, shares or deferred shares in lieu of fees, and restricted stock to nonemployee directors, advisors, and
consultants. The Company originally reserved a total of 3,384,433 shares of common stock to be issued under the Incentive Plan and the Nonemployee Plan. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In
October&nbsp;2005, the Company's board of directors approved the 2005 Long-Term Incentive Plan (the 2005 Plan), which was approved by the stockholders and became
effective in January&nbsp;2006. This plan provides for the grant of stock options, stock appreciation rights, restricted stock, restricted stock units and other equity-based awards. No additional
awards will be made under the Company's 1999 Long-Term Incentive Plan or the 1999 Non-Employee's Stock Plan (the 1999 plans) following the effective date of the 2005 Plan. Upon
effectiveness, 1,750,000 shares of common stock became reserved </FONT></P>

<P ALIGN="CENTER"><FONT SIZE=2>F-27</FONT></P>

<HR NOSHADE>
<P style='page-break-before:always'></p>

<A NAME="page_jq2221_1_28"> </A>
<BR>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>for
issuance under the 2005 Plan, as well as the shares remaining available for grants under the 1999 plans, which was 75,895 shares at December&nbsp;31, 2005. In addition, the plan contains an
"evergreen provision" which allows for an annual increase in the number of shares available for issuance under the plan on the first day of each of the Company's fiscal years beginning in fiscal year
2007. The annual increase in the number of shares shall be equal to the lesser of: (i)&nbsp;350,000 shares; (ii)&nbsp;1% of the Company's outstanding shares of common stock on the first day of the
fiscal year; or (iii)&nbsp;an amount determined by the Company's board of directors. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;For
certain grants issued during the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005, the Company has determined that the fair value of the underlying common stock on the date of the
grant was in excess of the exercise price of the options. As a result, the Company recorded deferred compensation on these stock options of approximately $1.8&nbsp;million as an increase in
additional paid-in capital and is amortizing it as a charge to operations over the vesting periods of four years. The Company recognized $0.1&nbsp;million in stock compensation expense
related to options issued to nonemployees for the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2003. There were no such option grants to non-employees in 2004 or 2005. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>Notes Receivable  </B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Certain members of management have been allowed to participate in an executive loan program. Included in Notes receivable from stockholders are purchase money
indebtedness notes issued in connection with the exercise of options granted under the Incentive Plan. Generally, the notes are with recourse and bear interest at a variable rate, which ranged from
3.62% to 6.25% at December&nbsp;31, 2004 and 4.08% to 8.25% at December&nbsp;31, 2005. During 2004, the Company issued a restricted stock purchase agreement for 432,214 shares of common stock to
an executive of the Company in exchange for a non-recourse note in the amount of $0.3&nbsp;million bearing interest at 4%. This note and accrued interest thereon was repaid in
August&nbsp;2005. All of the notes are collateralized by the underlying shares and the Company has repurchase rights for any exercised but unvested shares. Such notes receivable were
$0.5&nbsp;million and $0.2&nbsp;million at December&nbsp;31, 2004, and 2005, respectively. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>Warrants issued in 2005 were as follows:</B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As
inducement for the April&nbsp;2005 Investor Guaranty Agreement guarantee, the Company issued warrants to the investors to purchase 233,331 shares of common stock at a price of $0.03
per share. Also in connection with the April&nbsp;2005 amendment to the senior secured credit facility, the Company issued a warrant to purchase 116,666 shares of common stock at a price of $0.03
per share. The warrants are fully vested and expire in April&nbsp;2010. The value assigned to the April&nbsp;2005 warrants was $1.7&nbsp;million which has been recorded as deferred financing
fees, and is being amortized over the life of the credit facility. The fair value of the warrants was estimated at the date of the issuance using the Black-Scholes option-pricing model assuming a
risk-free interest rate of 3.9%, dividend yield of 0%, volatility of 65%, and expected life of the warrants of 5&nbsp;years. In August&nbsp;2005, 17,565 of these warrants were
exercised. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In
August&nbsp;2005 the Company issued 66,666 fully-vested, nonforfeitable common stock warrants at an exercise price of $0.75 per share to settle a dispute with an investor concerning
the conversion of its Series&nbsp;A preferred stock warrant and canceled its previously issued warrant to purchase 20,000 shares of common stock. The warrants expire in March 2008. The value
assigned to the warrant was $0.8&nbsp;million which was recognized as a legal settlement expense in the second quarter of 2005 when the contingency was settled in principle. The fair value of the
warrants was estimated at the date of the issuance using the Black-Scholes option-pricing model assuming a risk-free interest rate of 3.9%, dividend yield of 0%, volatility of 65%, and
expected life of the warrants of 3&nbsp;years. </FONT></P>

preferred stock at a price of $2.00 per share in </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>13.&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Commitments and Contingencies  </B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>Leases  </B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Company leases office space for its corporate headquarters as well as offices for its sales and operations activities in multiple markets across the United
States. The leases terminate at various dates through April&nbsp;2011 and generally provide for scheduled rent increases. Rental expense for all operating leases approximated $1.8&nbsp;million,
$1.9&nbsp;million, and $2.0&nbsp;million in 2003, 2004, and 2005, respectively. </FONT></P>


<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Our
principal executive offices are located in Wayne, Pennsylvania, where we lease approximately 34,600 square feet under a lease that expires in October&nbsp;31, 2010. These offices
are used for sales and marketing, back office processing, business development, storage of our equipment and technology, radio and television production and general corporate and finance activities. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The
Company leases office space under noncancelable operating lease agreements. The leases generally provide for scheduled rent increases. Future minimum lease payments under
noncancelable operating leases as of December&nbsp;31, 2005, are as follows: </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>Legal Proceedings  </B></FONT></P>


<P><FONT SIZE=2><B><I>Santa Fe Technologies v. Argus Networks,&nbsp;Inc.  </I></B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On July&nbsp;28, 1999, Santa Fe Technologies,&nbsp;Inc. ("SFT"), a traffic-sensor installation company, filed a complaint in a New Mexico state court against
the Company and others, including certain of the Company's officers, directors and TL Ventures LLC, whose successor, TL Ventures L.P., is affiliated with certain of our investors. The complaint sought
compensatory and punitive damages. The complaint related to a proposed merger between SFT and the Company which had been proposed in contemplation of its participation as a subcontractor in a team
proposal in response to a federal contract solicitation. In its complaint, SFT alleged that the Company and the other defendants conspired to and did misappropriate or misuse SFT's "corporate
opportunity," confidential information and work product, and committed conversion of SFT's intellectual property and contributions in order to win the award of the federal contract. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;During
the pre-trial phase of the case and the course of the trial, except for three causes of action against the Company and two causes of action against the investor
affiliate, all of the causes of action against all of the defendants were dismissed. The remaining claims consisted of a claim against the Company for breach of a claimed fiduciary duty not to use
information or "work product" shared in confidence, a related civil conspiracy claim against the Company and the investor affiliate and a claim against the Company and the investor affiliate for
unjust enrichment. The district court submitted the fiduciary duty claim (and the related conspiracy claim) to the jury for determination and decided the unjust enrichment claim itself. In
June&nbsp;2005, the fiduciary duty and conspiracy claims were tried before a jury. In late June&nbsp;2005, the jury returned a verdict against the Company on the breach of fiduciary duty claim and
the related conspiracy claim and against the investor affiliate on the conspiracy claim finding, that the Company and the investor affiliate are jointly and severally liable to plaintiff for
$6.2&nbsp;million in compensatory damages, assessing $5.0&nbsp;million in punitive damages against the Company and assessing punitive damages against the investor affiliate. The district court
subsequently rejected the plaintiff's unjust enrichment claim. On August&nbsp;19, 2005, the district court entered judgment against the Company on the breach of fiduciary duty and conspiracy claims
and against the investor affiliate on the conspiracy claim. In late September&nbsp;2005, the district court rejected the Company's motion to set aside the verdict. On October&nbsp;13, 2005, final
judgment was entered against the Company and the investor affiliate. On November&nbsp;17, 2005, the Company, Santa Fe Technologies,&nbsp;Inc. and TL Ventures L.P. entered into a settlement
agreement with respect to the litigation. Under this agreement, the Company and TL Ventures L.P. agreed to pay to SFT an aggregate of $14.25&nbsp;million in settlement of the litigation,
one-half to be paid within 30&nbsp;days of the date of the settlement agreement and the other half to be paid within 60&nbsp;days of the date of the settlement agreement. Upon receipt
of the full settlement amount, SFT has agreed to execute a general release of the Company and TL Ventures L.P. and all of our respective officers, directors, agents, employees, limited partners,
general partners, members and affiliates and to dismiss the litigation. Also on November&nbsp;17, 2005, the Company entered into an agreement with TL Ventures L.P. specifying that each party will
pay one-half of the settlement amount, or $7.125&nbsp;million. TL Ventures L.P. made the first settlement payment to SFT in December&nbsp;2005 and the Company made the second
settlement payment to SFT in January&nbsp;2006. In accordance with the SEC Staff Accounting Bulletin Topic 5T, </FONT><FONT SIZE=2><I>Accounting for Expense or Liabilities Paid by Principal
Stockholder(s)</I></FONT><FONT SIZE=2>, the Company has recognized the full value of the settlement of $14.25&nbsp;million in its statement of operations in legal settlements expense in the quarter
ended September&nbsp;30, 2005 and has treated the amounts paid by the principal stockholder ($7.125&nbsp;million) as a capital contribution. Accordingly, the liability related to the settlement of
this contingency recorded in the Company's balance sheet at December&nbsp;31, 2005 is the $7.125&nbsp;million paid in January&nbsp;2006. </FONT></P>

<P ALIGN="CENTER"><FONT SIZE=2>F-32</FONT></P>

<HR NOSHADE>
<P style='page-break-before:always'></p>
<!-- ZEQ.=1,SEQ=111,EFW="2168369",CP="TRAFFIC.COM, INC.",DN="1",CHK=534152,FOLIO='F-32',FILE='DISK133:[06NYC1.06NYC2221]JR2221A.;3',USER='JGUERRE',CD='23-MAR-2006;15:58' -->
<A NAME="page_jr2221_1_33"> </A>
<BR>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B><I>Traffic.com,&nbsp;Inc. v. Internet Capital Group,&nbsp;Inc. et al.  </I></B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On June&nbsp;29, 2005, the Company filed a complaint in the Chester County Pennsylvania Court of Common Pleas seeking a declaratory judgment against Internet
Capital Group,&nbsp;Inc., ICG Holdings,&nbsp;Inc. and related entities, or ICG, that the conversion of ICG's Series&nbsp;B convertible preferred stock to common stock on a
one-for-five basis (from approximately 5&nbsp;million shares of Series&nbsp;B preferred stock to approximately 1&nbsp;million shares of common stock), in connection with
the Company's Series&nbsp;E preferred stock financing in March&nbsp;2003, is valid and that ICG is not
entitled to any additional ownership stake in the Company or any other payments or damages in connection with the Series&nbsp;E preferred stock financing or related matters. On July&nbsp;26, 2005,
ICG filed preliminary objections to the Company's complaint for declaratory judgment which, under Pennsylvania practice, is equivalent to a motion to dismiss our complaint. On October&nbsp;28, 2005,
the Company and ICG entered into a settlement with respect to the litigation. Under this agreement, in exchange for mutual releases and an agreement to dismiss the litigation, the Company issued to
ICG 300,562 shares of common stock and agreed to allow ICG to grant to the underwriters in this offering the right to a specified number shares of the Company's common stock owned by ICG, within
30&nbsp;days after the date of this prospectus, to cover any over-allotments. As a result of this settlement, the Company recognized an expense in the second quarter of 2005 of
$3.5&nbsp;million in legal settlements expenses. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B><I>Pennsylvania Early Stage Partners, L.P.  </I></B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;On April&nbsp;22, 1999, the Company issued to Pennsylvania Early Stage Partners, L.P. ("PAES") a warrant to purchase 300,000 shares of the Company's
Series&nbsp;A preferred stock at an exercise price of $1.00 per share. Since May&nbsp;2004, PAES asserted that it remained entitled to purchase 100,000 shares of common stock, notwithstanding the
one-for-five recapitalization of the Company in connection with the Series&nbsp;E and E-1 preferred stock financing on March&nbsp;31, 2003. Following over a
year of disagreement and negotiations, on August&nbsp;24, 2005, the Company and PAES entered into a settlement agreement pursuant to which PAES' original warrant was terminated and exchanged for a
warrant to purchase 66,666 shares of common stock at an exercise price of $0.75 per share, the price at which similar warrants were issued at the time of the recapitalization, and both parties signed
mutual releases. As a result of this settlement, the Company recognized an expense in the second quarter of 2005 of $0.8&nbsp;million in legal settlements expenses. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B><I>Other Legal Proceedings  </I></B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Company is also subject to various other lawsuits and claims with respect to matters arising out of the normal course of business. While the impact on future
financial results for these other lawsuits and claims is not subject to reasonable estimation because considerable uncertainty exists, management believes, after consulting with counsel, that the
ultimate liabilities resulting from such other lawsuits and claims will not materially affect the results of operations or financial position of the Company. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>14.&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Related Party Transactions  </B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The Company has entered into an employment agreement with its chief executive officer that provides for a bonus of 3% of the total gross proceeds of any
transaction should such transaction result in the transfer of greater than 50% of the outstanding voting power of the Company. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Additionally,
the Company has employment agreements with two former executives that provide, in the aggregate, a combined bonus of 3.7% of the net proceeds of any transaction should such
transaction result in the transfer of greater than 50% of the outstanding voting power of the Company. These agreements expire in June 2006 and January 2007. </FONT></P>

<!-- ZEQ.=1,SEQ=113,EFW="2168369",CP="TRAFFIC.COM, INC.",DN="1",CHK=307313,FOLIO='F-34',FILE='DISK133:[06NYC1.06NYC2221]JS2221A.;4',USER='JGUERRE',CD='23-MAR-2006;15:58' -->
<A NAME="page_js2221_1_35"> </A>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>17.&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Subsequent Events  </B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>Initial Public Offering  </B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In January&nbsp;2006 the Company offered 6,550,000 shares of common stock during its Initial Public Offering which resulted in proceeds, after the underwriters'
discount and offering costs, of approximately $70.9&nbsp;million. On January&nbsp;25, 2006, the Company began trading on the NASDAQ national market under the symbol "TRFC". </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In
February&nbsp;2006, the underwriters' elected to exercise a portion of their over allotment option and offered an additional 400,000 shares. Of the additional shares, 337,666 were
offered by the Company
and 62,334 by selling stockholders. The Company realized proceeds of $3.8&nbsp;million, net of the underwriters' discount. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Upon
the closing of the initial public offering, the outstanding shares of redeemable convertible preferred stock, with a carrying value of $57.0&nbsp;million, were converted into
9,242,312&nbsp;shares of common stock, in accordance with their terms. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>Senior Secured Credit Facility  </B></FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In January&nbsp;2006 the Company retired its senior secured credit facility by paying $37.7&nbsp;million of outstanding principal and accrued interest using a
portion of the proceeds from the initial public offering. </FONT></P>

<P><FONT SIZE=2><B>Revolving Credit Facility  </B></FONT></P>


<P><FONT SIZE=2>&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In February&nbsp;2006, the Company repaid the outstanding obligation due under the revolving credit facility with a portion of the proceeds from the initial
public offering. In March&nbsp;2006, the facility expired upon its terms. </FONT></P>

<P ALIGN="CENTER"><FONT SIZE=2>F-35</FONT></P>
